=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Takahashi and Aoki will play a game using cards with numbers written on them.
Initially, Takahashi has N cards with numbers A_1, \ldots, A_N in his hand, Aoki has M cards with numbers B_1, \ldots, B_M in his hand, and there are L cards with numbers C_1, \ldots, C_L on the table.
Throughout the game, both Takahashi and Aoki know all the numbers on all the cards, including the opponent's hand.
Starting with Takahashi, they take turns performing the following action:

- Choose one card from his hand and put it on the table. Then, if there is a card on the table with a number less than the number on the card he just played, he may take one such card from the table into his hand.

The player who cannot make a move first loses, and the other player wins. Determine who wins if both players play optimally.
It can be proved that the game always ends in a finite number of moves.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M L
A_1 \ldots A_N
B_1 \ldots B_M
C_1 \ldots C_L

Output

Print Takahashi if Takahashi wins, and Aoki if Aoki wins.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N, M, L
- N + M + L \leq 12
- 1 \leq A_i, B_i, C_i \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

1 1 2
2
4
1 3

Sample Output 1

Aoki

The game may proceed as follows (not necessarily optimal moves):

- Takahashi plays 2 from his hand to the table, and takes 1 from the table into his hand. Now, Takahashi's hand is (1), Aoki's hand is (4), and the table cards are (2,3).
- Aoki plays 4 from his hand to the table, and takes 2 into his hand. Now, Takahashi's hand is (1), Aoki's hand is (2), and the table cards are (3,4).
- Takahashi plays 1 from his hand to the table. Now, Takahashi's hand is (), Aoki's hand is (2), and the table cards are (1,3,4).
- Aoki plays 2 from his hand to the table. Now, Takahashi's hand is (), Aoki's hand is (), and the table cards are (1,2,3,4).
- Takahashi cannot make a move and loses; Aoki wins.

Sample Input 2

4 4 4
98 98765 987654 987654321
987 9876 9876543 98765432
123 12345 1234567 123456789

Sample Output 2

Takahashi

Sample Input 3

1 1 8
10
10
1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8

Sample Output 3

Aoki

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's explore possible state-space reduction. Since total cards <=12, we can use bitmask.

Input format: N M L on first line, then A, B, C. Need combine all cards. We don't actually need N/M/L except K. But for initial hands: Takahashi hand mask of first N cards, Aoki hand mask of next M, table mask of last L. Values list.

Recursive function:

from functools import lru_cache

@lru_cache(None)
def dfs(tmask, amask, turn):
    # current hand
    hand = tmask if turn == 0 else amask
    if hand == 0:
        return False
    opp = amask if turn == 0 else tmask
    table = allmask ^ (tmask | amask)
    # iterate bits in hand
    h = hand
    while h:
        lsb = h & -h
        i = lsb.bit_length() - 1
        h -= lsb
        # remove i from current hand
        new_hand = hand ^ lsb
        # table after playing i = table | lsb
        # lower candidates = table & lower_mask[i]
        cand = table & lower_mask[i]
        # no take:
        # update state: if turn T, new tmask = new_hand, amask unchanged; if turn A, new amask = new_hand, tmask unchanged
        if turn == 0:
            nt = new_hand
            na = amask
        else:
            nt = tmask
            na = new_hand
        if not dfs(nt, na, 1-turn):
            return True
        # take each candidate
        c = cand
        while c:
            lb = c & -c
            j = lb.bit_length() - 1
            c -= lb
            # take j from table into current hand
            new_hand_take = new_hand | lb
            if turn == 0:
                nt = new_hand_take
                na = amask
            else:
                nt = tmask
                na = new_hand_take
            if not dfs(nt, na, 1-turn):
                return True
    return False

Need ensure table after playing i includes old table + i. Candidate lower cards are old table with val < val[i]. But what if old table contains i? No, i was in hand, not table. Good. What if there are duplicate values and another card with same value in table; not lower, cannot take. Good.

Could implement dfs recursively but with K<=12, maybe depth not too high. Let's try to bound depth more tightly. Each move either:
- no take: current hand count decreases by 1.
- take: current hand count stays same, but current hand sum decreases by at least 1 (integer values).
The total number of cards in current player's hand can stay same many times. But can a player take many times in a row? No, turns alternate. But a player may take on each of their turns, keeping hand count same, while opponent maybe also takes or no-take. Could depth be large? Let's construct with K=12. Suppose each player has one card, table has 10 cards. Each turn player plays their hand card and takes a lower table card, so hand count remains 1. Table count remains constant? Play one, take one: table count unchanged. But the played high card remains on table, and the taken lower card moves to hand. Over time, high cards accumulate on table, low cards cycle through hands. sum_val = [0]* (1<<K)
for mask in range(1, 1<<K):

    i = lsb.bit_length()-1
    prev = mask ^ lsb
    sum_base[mask] = sum_base[prev] + pow3[i]
    sum_val[mask] = sum_val[prev] + val[i]

total_val = sum_val[allmask]

Let's derive transitions with table_mask representation.

State: table_mask Tab, tmask Tak, amask Aok. Turn current player P.

Move: choose card i in current hand.
- Remove i from current hand. Table becomes Tab | bit_i (because i placed).
- If take j from old table with val[j] < val[i], then j removed from table and added to current hand. Table becomes (Tab | bit_i) & ~bit_j = (Tab & ~bit_j) | bit_i (since j in Tab, i not in Tab). If no take: Tab | bit_i.
- Current hand becomes (hand_without_i) | (bit_j if take else 0).
- Opponent hand unchanged.

Then ncode = 2*sum_base[new_Tab] + sum_base[new_Aok] (because Aoki hand digit 1; Takahashi digit 0). Note if turn=0, new_Aok = old Aok. If turn=1, new_Aok = new Aoki hand. This is elegant.

Need ensure bit_i not in Tab before move (it's in hand), so Tab | bit_i valid. bit_j in Tab before move, so removing okay.

Now, code details.

Precompute:
K = N + M + L
vals = A + B + C
bit = [1<<i for i in range(K)]
allmask = (1<<K) - 1

pow3 = [1]*K
for i in range(1,K): pow3[i] = pow3[i-1]*3
size = pow3[K-1]*3? Actually 3**K. Use 3**K.

sum_base = [0]*(1<<K)
sum_val = [0]*(1<<K)
for mask in range(1, 1<<K):
    lsb = mask & -mask
    i = lsb.bit_length()-1
    prev = mask ^ lsb
    sum_base[mask] = sum_base[prev] + pow3[i]
    sum_val[mask] = sum_val[prev] + vals[i]

lower_mask = [0]*K
for i in range(K):
    m=0
    for j in range(K):
        if vals[j] < vals[i]:
            m |= bit[j]
    lower_mask[i]=m

table_masks = list(range(1<<K))
table_masks.sort(key=lambda m: sum_val[m], reverse=True)

win0 = bytearray(size)
win1 = bytearray(size)

Process:
for Tab in table_masks:
    avail = allmask ^ Tab
    # enumerate Tak subset of avail
    Tak = avail
    while True:
        Aok = avail ^ Tak
        code = 2 * sum_base[Tab] + sum_base[Aok]
        # turn 0
        if Tak:
            # compute moves
            hand = Tak
            # maybe local variables for speed
            # We'll write loops.
        # turn 1
        if Aok:
            ...
        if Tak == 0:
            break
        Tak = (Tak - 1) & avail

Need be careful enumeration order: Starting Tak=avail, then submasks descending, includes 0. The while loop with break when Tak==0 after processing. Good.

But note code = 2*sum_base[Tab] + sum_base[Aok]. If Tak=avail, Aok=0. If Tak=0, Aok=avail. Good.

Now, computing moves. Need local variables for speed:
sb = sum_base
wm0 = win0; wm1 = win1
lower = lower_mask
bits = bit

For turn0:
    hand = Tak
    h = hand
    while h:
        lsb = h & -h
        i = lsb.bit_length() - 1
        h ^= lsb
        # no take
        new_Tab = Tab | lsb
        ncode = 2 * sb[new_Tab] + sb[Aok]
        if wm1[ncode] == 0:
            wm0[code] = 1
            break
        # take
        cand = Tab & lower[i]
        c = cand
        while c:
            j_lsb = c & -c
            c ^= j_lsb
            new_Tab2 = (Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb
            ncode = 2 * sb[new_Tab2] + sb[Aok]
            if wm1[ncode] == 0:
                wm0[code] = 1
                break
        if wm0[code]:
            break
    # If no winning move, remains 0.

Need note: In no take, new_Tab = Tab | lsb. Since lsb is bit i. But what if i was in hand, so not in Tab. Good. For take, new_Tab = (Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb. Since j_lsb in Tab. Good. But if j_lsb == lsb? impossible because lsb not in Tab, j_lsb in Tab. Good.

For turn1:
    hand = Aok
    h = hand
    while h:
        lsb = h & -h
        i = lsb.bit_length() - 1
        h ^= lsb
        # no take
        new_Tab = Tab | lsb
        new_Aok = Aok & ~lsb
        ncode = 2 * sb[new_Tab] + sb[new_Aok]
        if wm0[ncode] == 0:
            wm1[code] = 1
            break
        cand = Tab & lower[i]
        c = cand
        while c:
            j_lsb = c & -c
            c ^= j_lsb
            new_Tab2 = (Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb
            new_Aok2 = (Aok & ~lsb) | j_lsb
            ncode = 2 * sb[new_Tab2] + sb[new_Aok2]
            if wm0[ncode] == 0:
                wm1[code] = 1
                break
        if wm1[code]:
            break

Need ensure if no take and Aok hand becomes empty, new_Aok = Aok & ~lsb. Good. If take, new_Aok includes j. Good.

Let's attempt to find a counterexample to "taking always wins if no-take wins" by considering parity. Suppose current has two cards, no-take leaves one card; take leaves two cards. Having an extra card means current will have to move one extra time. If opponent has no cards, current wins anyway. If opponent has cards, extra moves can change who makes last move. In normal play, making the last move wins. If you have an extra card, you will make more moves; but opponent also moves after each of your moves. The total number of moves parity matters. Taking a card from table reduces table cards, maybe reduces opponent's future options. It might be bad if the extra card is very low and gives opponent a chance to take a high card? But opponent can only take lower than their played card; low card in your hand doesn't help opponent until you play it, and when you play it, it may be lower than opponent's future cards, allowing opponent to take it. But if you didn't take it, it would remain on table and opponent might take it anyway. Actually if you leave y on table, opponent could take y on their turn if they play >y. If you take y into hand, you deny it until you play it; but eventually you may play it, then opponent can take it. So taking y delays opponent's access to y, likely good. It also gives you an extra move. Seems weakly good.

But there might be zugzwang where having an extra move is bad because you would prefer to have no hand and make opponent move? In normal play, having an extra legal move is generally not monotonic in impartial games? Actually in normal play, if you add a move to your options, the outcome can change from losing to winning or from winning to losing? Let's recall: In impartial combinatorial games, adding an option can be bad because the option may be to a winning state for opponent, but you are not forced to take it. Adding options cannot hurt in terms of winning/losing for the player to move? Wait in normal play, if you have more options, you have a superset of moves. If the original set had no winning move, adding a losing move doesn't help; adding a winning move helps. If original set had a winning move, still winning. So adding legal moves cannot turn a winning state into losing. But here "taking" doesn't add an option to the same state; it leads to a different state. It's not adding an option to the current state; it's changing the resulting state. So no monotonicity.

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, M, L = data[0], data[1], data[2]
    p = 3
    A = data[p:p+N]; p += N
    B = data[p:p+M]; p += M
    C = data[p:p+L]
    vals = A + B + C
    K = N + M + L
    allmask = (1 << K) - 1

    bit_to_idx = [0] * (1 << K)
    for i in range(K):
        bit_to_idx[1 << i] = i

    pow3 = [1] * K
    for i in range(1, K):
        pow3[i] = pow3[i-1] * 3
    size = pow3[K-1] * 3 if K else 1  # K>=1

    sum_base = [0] * (1 << K)
    sum_val = [0] * (1 << K)
    for mask in range(1, 1 << K):
        lsb = mask & -mask
        i = bit_to_idx[lsb]
        prev = mask ^ lsb
        sum_base[mask] = sum_base[prev] + pow3[i]
        sum_val[mask] = sum_val[prev] + vals[i]

    sb2 = [2 * x for x in sum_base]

    lower_mask = [0] * K
    for i in range(K):
        m = 0
        vi = vals[i]
        for j in range(K):
            if vals[j] < vi:
                m |= 1 << j
        lower_mask[i] = m

    table_masks = list(range(1 << K))
    table_masks.sort(key=lambda m: sum_val[m], reverse=True)

    win0 = bytearray(size)
    win1 = bytearray(size)

    for Tab in table_masks:
        avail = allmask ^ Tab
        base_tab = sb2[Tab]
        Tak = avail
        while True:
            Aok = avail ^ Tak
            code = base_tab + sb[Aok]  # Need sb variable? We have sum_base. Use sum_base.
            if Tak:
                h = Tak
                while h:
                    lsb = h & -h
                    h ^= lsb
                    i = bit_to_idx[lsb]
                    # no take
                    ncode = sb2[Tab | lsb] + sum_base[Aok]
                    if win1[ncode] == 0:
                        win0[code] = 1
                        break
                    cand = Tab & lower_mask[i]
                    c = cand
                    while c:
                        j_lsb = c & -c
                        c ^= j_lsb
                        ncode = sb2[(Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb] + sum_base[Aok]
                        if win1[ncode] == 0:
                            win0[code] = 1
                            break
                    if win0[code]:
                        break
            if Aok:
                h = Aok
                while h:
                    lsb = h & -h
                    h ^= lsb
                    i = bit_to_idx[lsb]
                    # no take
                    ncode = sb2[Tab | lsb] + sum_base[Aok & ~lsb]
                    if win0[ncode] == 0:
                        win1[code] = 1
                        break
                    cand = Tab & lower_mask[i]
                    c = cand
                    while c:
                        j_lsb = c & -c
                        c ^= j_lsb
                        ncode = sb2[(Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb] + sum_base[(Aok & ~lsb) | j_lsb]
                        if win0[ncode] == 0:
                            win1[code] = 1
                            break
                    if win1[code]:
                        break
            if Tak == 0:
                break
            Tak = (Tak - 1) & avail

    Tak0 = (1 << N) - 1
    Aok0 = ((1 << M) - 1) << N
    Tab0 = allmask ^ Tak0 ^ Aok0
    code0 = sb2[Tab0] + sum_base[Aok0]
    print("Takahashi" if win0[code0] else "Aoki")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check variable `sb` used? In code above I used `sum_base` in loops. To improve speed, assign local `sb = sum_base`; `wm0=win0; wm1=win1; lower=lower_mask; idx=bit_to_idx; sb2_local=sb2`. But inside main, local variables already fast. We can use `sb = sum_base` for shorter. Let's write with local aliases for readability and speed.

We don't need to skip turn1. Good.

But note: If turn0 hand loop breaks due to winning move, we exit `while h` and then go to `if Aok:`. Good.

    ...
    if win1[ncode] == 0:
        win0[code] = 1
        break
    cand...
    while c:
        ...
        if win1[ncode] == 0:
            win0[code] = 1
            break
    if win0[code]:
        break
This breaks outer while h if win0 set. Good.

But if no-take wins, we break immediately without considering other cards. That's fine because one winning move enough. Then after while h, we proceed to turn1. Good.

Let's write the final code carefully:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, M, L = data[0], data[1], data[2]
    p = 3
    A = data[p:p+N]; p += N
    B = data[p:p+M]; p += M
    C = data[p:p+L]
    vals = A + B + C
    K = N + M + L
    allmask = (1 << K) - 1

    bit_to_idx = [0] * (1 << K)
    for i in range(K):
        bit_to_idx[1 << i] = i

    pow3 = [1] * K
    for i in range(1, K):
        pow3[i] = pow3[i-1] * 3
    size = 3 ** K

    sum_base = [0] * (1 << K)
    sum_val = [0] * (1 << K)
    for mask in range(1, 1 << K):
        lsb = mask & -mask
        i = bit_to_idx[lsb]
        prev = mask ^ lsb
        sum_base[mask] = sum_base[prev] + pow3[i]
        sum_val[mask] = sum_val[prev] + vals[i]

    sb2 = [2 * x for x in sum_base]

    lower_mask = [0] * K
    for i in range(K):
        vi = vals[i]
        m = 0
        for j in range(K):
            if vals[j] < vi:
                m |= 1 << j
        lower_mask[i] = m

    table_masks = list(range(1 << K))
    table_masks.sort(key=lambda m: sum_val[m], reverse=True)

    win0 = bytearray(size)
    win1 = bytearray(size)

    sb = sum_base
    idx = bit_to_idx
    lower = lower_mask
    w0 = win0
    w1 = win1
    s2 = sb2

    for Tab in table_masks:
        avail = allmask ^ Tab
        base_tab = s2[Tab]
        Tak = avail
        while True:
            Aok = avail ^ Tak
            code = base_tab + sb[Aok]

            if Tak:
                h = Tak
                while h:
                    lsb = h & -h
                    h ^= lsb
                    i = idx[lsb]

                    ncode = s2[Tab | lsb] + sb[Aok]
                    if w1[ncode] == 0:
                        w0[code] = 1
                        break

                    cand = Tab & lower[i]
                    c = cand
                    while c:
                        j_lsb = c & -c
                        c ^= j_lsb
                        ncode = s2[(Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb] + sb[Aok]
                        if w1[ncode] == 0:
                            w0[code] = 1
                            break
                    if w0[code]:
                        break

            if Aok:
                h = Aok
                while h:
                    lsb = h & -h
                    h ^= lsb
                    i = idx[lsb]

                    ncode = s2[Tab | lsb] + sb[Aok & ~lsb]
                    if w0[ncode] == 0:
                        w1[code] = 1
                        break

                    cand = Tab & lower[i]
                    c = cand
                    while c:
                        j_lsb = c & -c
                        c ^= j_lsb
                        ncode = s2[(Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb] + sb[(Aok & ~lsb) | j_lsb]
                        if w0[ncode] == 0:
                            w1[code] = 1
                            break
                    if w1[code]:
                        break

            if Tak == 0:
                break
            Tak = (Tak - 1) & avail

    Tak0 = (1 << N) - 1
    Aok0 = ((1 << M) - 1) << N
    Tab0 = allmask ^ Tak0 ^ Aok0
    code0 = s2[Tab0] + sb[Aok0]
    print("Takahashi" if w0[code0] else "Aoki")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

But we did not break out of the `while True` state loop, which is correct because we still need to compute win1 for this state. However, the `while h` loop is exited; then after turn1, we reach `if Tak == 0: break` etc. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ncode = s2[(Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb] + sb[Aok]" for turn0 take. If `(Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb` and Aok disjoint,
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, M, L = data[0], data[1], data[2]
    p = 3
    A = data[p:p+N]
    p += N
    B = data[p:p+M]
    p += M
    C = data[p:p+L]

    vals = A + B + C
    K = N + M + L
    allmask = (1 << K) - 1

    bit_to_idx = [0] * (1 << K)
    for i in range(K):
        bit_to_idx[1 << i] = i

    pow3 = [1] * K
    for i in range(1, K):
        pow3[i] = pow3[i-1] * 3
    size = 3 ** K

    sum_base = [0] * (1 << K)
    sum_val = [0] * (1 << K)
    for mask in range(1, 1 << K):
        lsb = mask & -mask
        i = bit_to_idx[lsb]
        prev = mask ^ lsb
        sum_base[mask] = sum_base[prev] + pow3[i]
        sum_val[mask] = sum_val[prev] + vals[i]

    sb2 = [2 * x for x in sum_base]

    lower_mask = [0] * K
    for i in range(K):
        m = 0
        for j in range(K):
            if vals[j] < vals[i]:
                m |= 1 << j
        lower_mask[i] = m

    table_masks = list(range(1 << K))
    table_masks.sort(key=lambda m: sum_val[m], reverse=True)

    win0 = bytearray(size)  # Takahashi's turn
    win1 = bytearray(size)  # Aoki's turn

    sb = sum_base
    s2 = sb2
    idx = bit_to_idx
    lower = lower_mask
    w0 = win0
    w1 = win1

    for Tab in table_masks:
        avail = allmask ^ Tab
        base_tab = s2[Tab]
        Tak = avail

        while True:
            Aok = avail ^ Tak
            code = base_tab + sb[Aok]

            if Tak:
                h = Tak
                while h:
                    lsb = h & -h
                    h ^= lsb
                    i = idx[lsb]

                    # Do not take a card
                    ncode = s2[Tab | lsb] + sb[Aok]
                    if w1[ncode] == 0:
                        w0[code] = 1
                        break

                    # Take one lower card from the table
                    cand = Tab & lower[i]
                    c = cand
                    while c:
                        j_lsb = c & -c
                        c ^= j_lsb
                        ncode = s2[(Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb] + sb[Aok]
                        if w1[ncode] == 0:
                            w0[code] = 1
                            break

                    if w0[code]:
                        break

            if Aok:
                h = Aok
                while h:
                    lsb = h & -h
                    h ^= lsb
                    i = idx[lsb]

                    # Do not take a card
                    ncode = s2[Tab | lsb] + sb[Aok & ~lsb]
                    if w0[ncode] == 0:
                        w1[code] = 1
                        break

                    # Take one lower card from the table
                    cand = Tab & lower[i]
                    c = cand
                    while c:
                        j_lsb = c & -c
                        c ^= j_lsb
                        ncode = s2[(Tab & ~j_lsb) | lsb] + sb[(Aok & ~lsb) | j_lsb]
                        if w0[ncode] == 0:
                            w1[code] = 1
                            break

                    if w1[code]:
                        break

            if Tak == 0:
                break
            Tak = (Tak - 1) & avail

    Tak0 = (1 << N) - 1
    Aok0 = ((1 << M) - 1) << N
    Tab0 = allmask ^ Tak0 ^ Aok0
    code0 = s2[Tab0] + sb[Aok0]

    print("Takahashi" if w0[code0] else "Aoki")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
